you wrote it, on this run's storage volume, at the path it already has — it is
not moved, renamed or copied. A later round reads it there, by that absolute
workspace path, so a checkpoint you keep is a checkpoint the next round can

your cwd to GitHub but skips every file of 100 MB or

<data_loading_pattern>
`mini_demo_data.json` = curated subset for the demo.
Use this pattern for Colab compatibility (GitHub URL with local fallback):
```python
GITHUB_DATA_URL = "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ai-inventor-papers/ai-invention-295f8e-prompt-bans-substitute-not-just-subtract/fork/run_jSassd-YWLVC/round-3/evaluation-2/demo/mini_demo_data.json"
import http.client
import json
import urllib.request
from pathlib import Path

def load_data():
    try:
        with urllib.request.urlopen(GITHUB_DATA_URL, timeout=15) as response:
            return json.loads(response.read().decode())
    # Network errors, timeouts, a placeholder URL, bad JSON, a cut-off response.
    except (OSError, ValueError, http.client.HTTPException) as e:
        print("GitHub data unavailable, using local mini_demo_data.json:", e)
    local = Path("mini_demo_data.json")
    if local.exists(): return json.loads(local.read_text())
    raise FileNotFoundError("Could not load mini_demo_data.json")
```
</data_loading_pattern>

take this repo as inspiration: https://github.com/DrejcPesjak/llm-creativity
use one or more open-weight models (minimum 1B params), probably instruction tuned it
and first run behavioral analysis (similar to repo mentioned above), then move to interpretability (can be manifolds, or also other techniques like sae, circuits, nla, j-lens, logit lens...)
the question is: how does "creativity" manifest inside the model, and can we influence it?
(in layman terms, can we make llms give truly creative and novel solutions?)

(you do not need to implement everything bellow, see whats reasonable, interesting and might lead to new insights)
is "temp" really the "llm creativity knob"?  (Astra found "coincidentally" similar direction for navier-stokes solution, as Buckmaster & Levent)
(always same seed)
1 a) does a tight temp , with topk=10 produce 10 reasoning trajectories of 1 idea/solution but same 10 phrasings
b) or does tight temp, with topk=10 produce 10 reasoning trajectories of 10 ideas/solutions with 1 phrasing each
c) and does loosing up temp, make a) more to 2/3/4 ideas each with 10 phrasings ; or make b) keep original 10 ideas, now each with 10 phrasings
(temp is rather a minor part in this idea, put more focus on the following things)
test: "implement path saerch algorithm" -> does llm always design BFS?
2 can context seeding help? prepand "seed=d32qxk4nt43" or "seed=(horse chair roof love)"
hyp: is llm a probablistic model, and thus gives you the average/mean answer? and only context pushes you into a different subspace
 - ie of possible range of answer [0,100] , on direct question llm gives you 50. then with context you move to [80,100] subspace and llm gives you 90
3 so how much does prompt instructions matter? ("Make a new algorithm", "Make a fully novel algorithm never seen before ... 2000 words ..."
-> analyze the internals , all the runs that produced BFS , vs all runs that produced DFS , vs dijkstra ...
  -> analyze with thought anchors, NLA, SAE ...
  -> what is the average answer? plot a "path search algorithm" manifold, color each algorithm centroid by implemented-count (lets say we have 10 algorithms in total of 100 gens)
     -> then prohibit the top 5 in prompt, and see if the implemented count moves to the edge of those 5, or to the opposite side (if its a circle manifold)
  - (manifold lit: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.05115 , https://www.goodfire.com/research/manifold-steering ) - geodesic and mds
    - (the reports need to contain actual geometry plots, actual activation space, not some summary bar charts)

{"stdout": "# Demo: do algorithm bans pick a substitute or a ladder? (FRLP screen on Qwen3-8B)\n\nThis folder turns the experiment artifact *\"Do algorithm bans pick a substitute or a ladder?\"* (round 2,\nexperiment 7) into a notebook that runs top to bottom in about two minutes on a 2-CPU machine (Google Colab or\nlocal Jupyter).\n\n**The question.** When a prompt bans an algorithm (\"Do not use BFS for this task\"), the probability mass that\nleaves it can go to two kinds of place:\n\n* a **specific substitute** chosen for that ban;\n* a **generic ladder**, i.e. the same profile that any perturbation of the prompt produces.\n\n**The instrument.** Forced-ratio latent probabilities (FRLP): importance-sampled estimates of\nlog P(model writes algorithm j | prompt), computed from teacher-forced log-likelihoods of forced, name-scrubbed\nprograms. The model is Qwen3-8B and there are two path-search tasks:\n\n* T1, grid path, with 6 algorithms;\n* T2, weighted shortest path, with 5 algorithms.\n\nThe notebook keeps the artifact's own code, split into cells with an explanation before each section:\n\n* `method.py`, the stage orchestrator;\n* `src/config.py`, the frozen protocol;\n* `src/frlp.py` and `src/analysis_misc.py`, the FRLP cells;\n* `src/gate.py`, the validity gate;\n* `src/c1.py`, the C1 share test, AIM, sensitivity, shams and two-step bans;\n* `audit/rederive.py`, the independent re-derivation;\n* `src/outputs.py`, the figures F1–F6.\n\n## Layout\n\n| path | what it is |\n|---|---|\n| `code_demo.ipynb` | The demo notebook, saved with the outputs of a complete run in a clean Python 3.12 environment. |\n| `mini_demo_data.json` | Demo data (1.5 MB): 99 of the 211 scored forced programs (the first 9 per algorithm by their pre-registered selection rank), each with raw and name-scrubbed code and its log-likelihoods `[total, body, cum256]` under every scored condition (17,625 scores). It also holds the side tables the analysis reads (yields, the 19 gate events with their sampled labels, free-form association profiles, prompt segments and nouns) and the full run's headline numbers. |\n| `build_mini_demo_data.py` | Rebuilds `mini_demo_data.json` from the artifact folder (`--m` sets the programs kept per algorithm). |\n| `logs/build_mini_demo_data.log` | Log of the data builds. |\n| `logs/nbtest_round{0,1,2}.log`, `logs/nbtest_final.log` | `nbconvert` test runs in a fresh venv: minimum, 10% and full-run statistical settings, then the final notebook (75 s). |\n| `.aii/manifest.yaml` | Storage manifest. It has no entries, because nothing in this folder is heavy. |\n\n## How to run\n\n* **Colab:** open `code_demo.ipynb` and run all cells. The first cell installs `loguru` and `rapidfuzz`, and the data is fetched from this repository's raw GitHub URL.\n* **Local (Python 3.12):**\n\n  ```bash\n  pip install jupyter ipykernel\n  jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute code_demo.ipynb --output code_demo.ipynb\n  ```\n\n  Outside Colab, the install cell also pins numpy 2.0.2, pandas 2.2.2, scipy 1.16.3 and matplotlib 3.10.0, which are Colab's versions. If the GitHub URL is unreachable, the notebook falls back to the local `mini_demo_data.json`.\n* **Rebuild the data** from the artifact folder (the folder holding `method.py`, `scores/`, `data/`, `results/`, `prompts/` and `logs/`; in the published repository it is the parent of this folder):\n\n  ```bash\n  uv run --no-project --python 3.12 --with pandas==2.2.2 --with pyarrow==18.1.0 --with loguru==0.7.3 \\\n      python build_mini_demo_data.py --src ..\n  ```\n\n## Configuration and measured runtime\n\nAll tunable parameters sit in one configuration cell. The bootstrap, permutation and placebo sizes are at the\nfull run's values. `M_SCORED` is the one parameter limited by the demo data: it is 9 here, against 20 in the full\nrun. ```
File: /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_3/art_3tH2xtoZ7Gj2/build_mini_demo_data.py

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Build mini_demo_data.json for code_demo.ipynb: the novelty audit (section 0c of eval.py) on 100 audited programs.

The demo programs are the full run's own selection, rebuilt with the artifact's code (common/rows.py normalises every
per-program record, sections/s0c_novelty.py: build_selection() picks the audit sets):

* all 78 programs of Set A (every unique correct out-of-catalogue program);
* all 12 programs of Set B_TOP (exp9 multi:TOP programs outside the banned set);
* 10 of the 138 programs of Set B (failing out-of-catalogue programs from ban conditions), picked by hand to cover
  different experiments, both models, and every way a final class is reached (A = B, tie-break, manual override).

Each example is one per-program record of an origin file, reduced to the fields normalise() reads. The metadata holds
what the two sections read besides those records: the other 387 records of the four exp9 multi-ban conditions (labels
only, code removed) for the avoidance tally, the task specs and catalogue reference programs (judge prompts, positive
controls, AST similarity), the cached judge responses of these items and the 15 controls, the manual-pass records, and
the full run's results for comparison.

Usage (from this folder; --src is the evaluation artifact's folder, which holds eval.py, common/, sections/, audit/ and
results/; its dependencies are found by that artifact's own common/paths.py: dep_root()):

    python build_mini_demo_data.py --src .. --out mini_demo_data.json
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import os
import shutil
import sys
import tempfile
import time
from collections import Counter
from pathlib import Path

sys.dont_write_bytecode = True   # never write __pycache__ into the (read-only) artifact folder

B_PICK = ["B|t1|a67007251481c4", "B|t2|a6621d538183f3", "B|t1|4f29c5bfdb2542", "B|t1|29f503734ad472",
          "B|t2|3d7786f848478a", "B|t1|8c44b412fa9c85", "B|t2|723f33fafe6bd7", "B|t2|56790bc835eb78",
          "B|t2|c911613f2d834b", "B|t2|f979dd396c54c4"]
MULTI = ("multi:TOP", "multi:ARC", "multi:SCATTER", "multi:TOP5")
# raw fields read by common/rows.py: normalise(); everything else in the origin records is dropped
RAW_KEYS = ("task", "task_id", "code", "code_raw", "code_scrubbed", "j", "label", "condition", "parse_ok", "decoding",
            "key", "T", "temperature", "thinking", "arm", "steering", "steer", "model", "template", "paraphrase_id",
            "banned_set", "banned", "passed", "code_sha256", "kind", "phase", "X", "sample_id", "runtime_label",
            "rt_label", "label_runtime", "ast_label", "label_ast", "judge_label", "label_judge", "variant", "algorithm",
            "forced_mode")
SUMMARY_KEYS = ("n_items", "counts_unique", "counts_with_duplicates", "selection_commit", "kappa_A_B", "kappa_ci95",
                "kappa_by_set", "kappa_collapsed_known_novel_degenerate", "agreement_collapsed",
                "confusion_A_rows_B_cols", "n_tiebreaks", "final_class_counts", "judge_only_class_counts",
                "manual_agreement_setA_exact", "manual_agreement_setA_collapsed", "manual_n",
                "manual_class_counts_setA", "controls", "controls_placebo_wrong_names", "controls_identity_both_judges",
                "controls_called_novel", "controls_gate", "novelty_answer", "instruction_driven_setA",
                "multiban_avoidance", "spend_usd_judges", "judges")
ITEM_KEYS = ("set", "task", "judge_A", "judge_B", "judge_C", "final_judge_class", "final_how", "final_class",
             "manual_class", "manual_override", "closest_known", "max_ast4_jaccard", "max_ast4_jaccard_ref")


def main() -> None:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--src", default="..", help="the evaluation artifact's folder (holds eval.py)")
    ap.add_argument("--out", default="mini_demo_data.json")
    args = ap.parse_args()
    SRC = Path(args.src).resolve()
    sys.path.insert(0, str(SRC))
    from common.paths import EXP, dep, read_json, read_jsonl, sha256_text   # noqa: E402 (the artifact's code)
    from common.rows import iter_rows, normalise                            # noqa: E402
    from sections import s0c_novelty as N                                    # noqa: E402

    t0 = time.time()
    rows = list(iter_rows(include_exp12_extra=True))
    sel = N.build_selection(rows)
    print(f"rows {len(rows)}; full selection {{'A': {len(sel['A'])}, 'B_TOP': {len(sel['B_TOP'])}, "
          f"'B': {len(sel['B'])}}} ({time.time() - t0:.1f} s)")
    picks = {it["item_id"]: it for it in sel["B"] if it["item_id"] in B_PICK}
    assert len(picks) == len(B_PICK), "a picked Set B item is missing from the rebuilt selection"
    items = sel["A"] + sel["B_TOP"] + [picks[i] for i in B_PICK]
    assert all(it["n_copies"] == 1 for it in items), "every demo item must have exactly one copy"
    keys = {it["dedup_key"]: it for it in items}

    raw_files: dict[tuple[str, str], list[dict]] = {}

    def raw(source: str, line: int) -> dict:
        aid, rel = source.split(":", 1)
        if (aid, rel) not in raw_files:
            exp = next(e for e, a in EXP.items() if a == aid and e != "exp3") if not rel.startswith("inputs/iter1/exp3") \
                else "exp3"
            raw_files[(aid, rel)] = read_jsonl(dep(exp, rel))
        return raw_files[(aid, rel)][line - 1]

    def keep(r: dict, drop_code: bool = False) -> dict:
        return {k: r[k] for k in RAW_KEYS if k in r and not (drop_code and k == "code")}

    examples, used = [], set()
    for r in rows:                                   # file order, so each origin file keeps its original line order
        it = keys.get(r["dedup_key"]) if r["dedup_key"] else None
        if it is None:
            continue
        aid, rel = r["source"].split(":", 1)
        rec = keep(raw(r["source"], r["line"]))
        chk = normalise(r["exp"], rel, r["line"] - 1, rec)
        assert all(chk[k] == r[k] for k in r), f"normalise() differs on the reduced record of {it['item_id']}"
        examples.append({"item_id": it["item_id"], "set": it["set"], "exp": r["exp"], "aid": aid, "rel": rel,
                         "line": r["line"], "pinned": r["exp"] == "exp9" and r["condition"] in MULTI, "record": rec})
        used.add((r["source"], r["line"]))
    order = {it["item_id"]: i for i, it in enumerate(items)}
    examples.sort(key=lambda e: order[e["item_id"]])
    assert len(examples) == len(items) == 100, len(examples)

    side = []
    for r in rows:
        if r["exp"] == "exp9" and r["condition"] in MULTI and (r["source"], r["line"]) not in used:
            aid, rel = r["source"].split(":", 1)
            rec = keep(raw(r["source"], r["line"]), drop_code=True)
            chk = normalise(r["exp"], rel, r["line"] - 1, rec)
            assert all(chk[k] == r[k] for k in ("exp", "task", "model", "condition", "banned_set", "label",
                                                 "label_fine", "passed")), "label changed without code"
            side.append({"exp": r["exp"], "aid": aid, "rel": rel, "line": r["line"], "record": rec})
    n_multi = Counter(r["condition"] for r in rows if r["exp"] == "exp9" and r["condition"] in MULTI)
    print(f"examples {len(examples)} ({dict(Counter(e['set'] for e in examples))}; pinned "
          f"{sum(e['pinned'] for e in examples)}); side rows {len(side)}; exp9 multi-ban rows {dict(n_multi)}")

    # dependency files read by the two sections: task specs, catalogue references (controls, AST similarity), bench specs
    files: dict[str, str] = {}

    def add(exp: str, rel: str) -> None:
        files[f"{EXP[exp]}/{rel}"] = dep(exp, rel).read_text()

    for d in ("t1_grid", "t2_wsp"):
        add("exp1", f"tasks/{d}/spec.txt")
        for exp in ("exp1", "exp9"):
            for p in sorted(dep(exp, f"tasks/{d}/references").glob("*.py")):
                add(exp, f"tasks/{d}/references/{p.name}")
    for d in sorted(dep("exp11", "bench").iterdir()):
        if d.is_dir():
            if (d / "spec.json").exists():
                add("exp11", f"bench/{d.name}/spec.json")
            for p in sorted((d / "references").glob("*.py")) if (d / "references").exists() else []:
                add("exp11", f"bench/{d.name}/references/{p.name}")

    # cached judge responses (the 'raw' text is dropped; the parsed JSON object is kept) and the manual pass
    controls = N.build_controls()
    ids = set(order) | {c["item_id"] for c in controls}
    cache_rows = [{k: v for k, v in r.items() if k != "raw"} for r in read_jsonl(SRC / "audit" / "judge_cache.jsonl")
                  if r["item_id"] in ids]
    cache = {(r["judge"], r["item_id"], r["prompt_sha"]): r for r in cache_rows}
    for it in controls + items:
        p = sha256_text(N.build_prompt(it["task"], it["code"]))
        assert ("A", it["item_id"], p) in cache and ("B", it["item_id"], p) in cache, f"no cached judge for {it['item_id']}"
    manual = [r for r in read_jsonl(SRC / "audit" / "manual_pass.jsonl") if r["item_id"] in ids]

    # check: the selection rebuilt from the materialised demo files is exactly the demo items
    tmp = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="demo_check_", dir=Path(args.out).resolve().parent))
    try:
        for rel, text in files.items():
            (tmp / rel).parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
            (tmp / rel).write_text(text)
        by_file: dict[tuple[str, str], list] = {}
        for e in examples + side:
            by_file.setdefault((e["aid"], e["rel"]), []).append(e)
        for (aid, rel), es in by_file.items():
            (tmp / aid / rel).parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
            with (tmp / aid / rel).open("w") as fh:
                for e in sorted(es, key=lambda x: x["line"]):
                    fh.write(json.dumps(e["record"]) + "\n")
        os.environ["AII_DEPS_ROOT"] = str(tmp)
        demo_rows = list(iter_rows(include_exp12_extra=True))
        dsel = N.build_selection(demo_rows)
        got = [it["item_id"] for it in dsel["A"] + dsel["B_TOP"] + dsel["B"]]
        assert sorted(got) == sorted(order), "selection on the demo files differs from the demo items"
        n_demo_multi = Counter(r["condition"] for r in demo_rows if r["exp"] == "exp9" and r["condition"] in MULTI)
        assert n_demo_multi == n_multi, (n_demo_multi, n_multi)
        print(f"check passed: the demo files reproduce the 100 selected items and all {sum(n_multi.values())} "
              f"multi-ban rows; {len(cache)} cached judge responses cover every item and control")
    finally:
        os.environ.pop("AII_DEPS_ROOT", None)
        shutil.rmtree(tmp, ignore_errors=True)

    na = read_json(SRC / "results" / "novelty_audit.json")
    jr = read_json(SRC / "results" / "novelty_judge_run.json")
    full_items = {r["item_id"]: {k: r.get(k) for k in ITEM_KEYS} for r in na["table"] if r["item_id"] in order}
    meta = {
        "description": ("Demo data for the novelty audit (section 0c) of the iteration-3 Step 0 evaluation "
                        "(art_3tH2xtoZ7Gj2, eval.py). Each example is one per-program record of an origin file, reduced "
                        "to the fields common/rows.py: normalise() reads; 'aid'/'rel' name the origin file "
                        "(<artifact_id>/<relative path>) and 'line' its line in the full file."),
        "selection": {"rule": "full Set A (78) + full Set B_TOP (12) + 10 hand-picked Set B items (of 138)",
                      "B_pick": B_PICK, "pinned": "exp9 multi-ban programs (always kept: the avoidance tally needs them)",
                      "full_run_counts_unique": sel["counts_unique"], "full_run_rows": len(rows)},
        "raw_fields_kept": list(RAW_KEYS),
        "files": files,
        "judge_cache": cache_rows,
        "manual_pass": manual,
        "side_rows": side,
        "side_rows_note": ("records of exp9 data/samples.jsonl in the conditions multi:TOP, multi:ARC, multi:SCATTER, "
                           "multi:TOP5 that are not demo programs; code removed (labels, pass flags and banned sets "
                           "unchanged), used only by the multi-ban avoidance tally"),
        "full_run_reference": {**{k: na[k] for k in SUMMARY_KEYS if k in na},
                               "judge_spend": jr["spend"], "commit_sha256": jr["commit_sha256"],
                               "section_seconds": {k: v for k, v in read_json(SRC / "results" / "timings.json").items()
                                                   if k in ("judges", "novelty")}},
        "full_run_items": full_items,
    }
    out = {"metadata": meta, "datasets": [{"dataset": "novelty_audit_programs", "examples": examples}]}
    Path(args.out).write_text(json.dumps(out, indent=1))
    print(f"wrote {args.out}: {Path(args.out).stat().st_size / 1e6:.2f} MB; {len(files)} dependency files, "
          f"{len(cache_rows)} judge responses, {len(manual)} manual-pass records ({time.time() - t0:.1f} s)")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

as N                                    # noqa: E402\n\n    t0 = time.time()\n    rows = list(iter_rows(include_exp12_extra=True))\n    sel = N.build_selection(rows)\n    print(f\"rows {len(rows)}; full selection {{'A': {len(sel['A'])}, 'B_TOP': {len(sel['B_TOP'])}, \"\n          f\"'B': {len(sel['B'])}}} ({time.time() - t0:.1f} s)\")\n    picks = {it[\"item_id\"]: it for it in sel[\"B\"] if it[\"item_id\"] in B_PICK}\n    assert len(picks) == len(B_PICK), \"a picked Set B item is missing from the rebuilt selection\"\n    items = sel[\"A\"] + sel[\"B_TOP\"] + [picks[i] for i in B_PICK]\n    assert all(it[\"n_copies\"] == 1 for it in items), \"every demo item must have exactly one copy\"\n    keys = {it[\"dedup_key\"]: it for it in items}\n\n    raw_files: dict[tuple[str, str], list[dict]] = {}\n\n    def raw(source: str, line: int) -> dict:\n        aid, rel = source.split(\":\", 1)\n        if (aid, rel) not in raw_files:\n            exp = next(e for e, a in EXP.items() if a == aid and e != \"exp3\") if not rel.startswith(\"inputs/iter1/exp3\") \\\n                else \"exp3\"\n            raw_files[(aid, rel)] = read_jsonl(dep(exp, rel))\n        return raw_files[(aid, rel)][line - 1]\n\n    def keep(r: dict, drop_code: bool = False) -> dict:\n        return {k: r[k] for k in RAW_KEYS if k in r and not (drop_code and k == \"code\")}\n\n    examples, used = [], set()\n    for r in rows:                                   # file order, so each origin file keeps its original line order\n        it = keys.get(r[\"dedup_key\"]) if r[\"dedup_key\"] else None\n        if it is None:\n            continue\n        aid, rel = r[\"source\"].split(\":\", 1)\n        rec = keep(raw(r[\"source\"], r[\"line\"]))\n        chk = normalise(r[\"exp\"], rel, r[\"line\"] - 1, rec)\n        assert all(chk[k] == r[k] for k in r), f\"normalise() differs on the reduced record of {it['item_id']}\"\n        examples.append({\"item_id\": it[\"item_id\"], \"set\": it[\"set\"], \"exp\": r[\"exp\"], \"aid\": aid, \"rel\": rel,\n                         \"line\": r[\"line\"], \"pinned\": r[\"exp\"] == \"exp9\" and r[\"condition\"] in MULTI, \"record\": rec})\n        used.add((r[\"source\"], r[\"line\"]))\n    order = {it[\"item_id\"]: i for i, it in enumerate(items)}\n    examples.sort(key=lambda e: order[e[\"item_id\"]])\n    assert len(examples) == len(items) == 100, len(examples)\n\n    side = []\n    for r in rows:\n        if r[\"exp\"] == \"exp9\" and r[\"condition\"] in MULTI and (r[\"source\"], r[\"line\"]) not in used:\n            aid, rel = r[\"source\"].split(\":\", 1)\n            rec = keep(raw(r[\"source\"], r[\"line\"]), drop_code=True)\n            chk = normalise(r[\"exp\"], rel, r[\"line\"] - 1, rec)\n            assert all(chk[k] == r[k] for k in (\"exp\", \"task\", \"model\", \"condition\", \"banned_set\", \"label\",\n                                                 \"label_fine\", \"passed\")), \"label changed without code\"\n            side.append({\"exp\": r[\"exp\"], \"aid\": aid, \"rel\": rel, \"line\": r[\"line\"], \"record\": rec})\n    n_multi = Counter(r[\"condition\"] for r in rows if r[\"exp\"] == \"exp9\" and r[\"condition\"] in MULTI)\n    print(f\"examples {len(examples)} ({dict(Counter(e['set'] for e in examples))}; pinned \"\n          f\"{sum(e['pinned'] for e in examples)}); side rows {len(side)}; exp9 multi-ban rows {dict(n_multi)}\")\n\n    # dependency files read by the two sections: task specs, catalogue references (controls, AST similarity), bench specs\n    files: dict[str, str] = {}\n\n    def add(exp: str, rel: str) -> None:\n        files[f\"{EXP[exp]}/{rel}\"] = dep(exp, rel).read_text()\n\n    for d in (\"t1_grid\", \"t2_wsp\"):\n        add(\"exp1\", f\"tasks/{d}/spec.txt\")\n        for exp in (\"exp1\", \"exp9\"):\n            for p in sorted(dep(exp, f\"tasks/{d}/references\").glob(\"*.py\")):\n                add(exp, f\"tasks/{d}/references/{p.name}\")\n    for d in sorted(dep(\"exp11\", \"bench\").iterdir()):\n        if d.is_dir():\n            if (d / \"spec.json\").exists():\n                add(\"exp11\", f\"bench/{d.name}/spec.json\")\n            for p in sorted((d / \"references\").glob(\"*.py\")) if (d / \"references\").exists() else []:\n                add(\"exp11\", f\"bench/{d.name}/references/{p.name}\")\n\n    # cached judge responses (the 'raw' text is dropped; the parsed JSON object is kept) and the manual pass\n    controls = N.build_controls()\n    ids = set(order) | {c[\"item_id\"] for c in controls}\n    cache_rows = [{k: v for k, v in r.items() if k != \"raw\"} for r in read_jsonl(SRC / \"audit\" / \"judge_cache.jsonl\")\n                  if r[\"item_id\"] in ids]\n    cache = {(r[\"judge\"], r[\"item_id\"], r[\"prompt_sha\"]): r for r in cache_rows}\n    for it in controls + items:\n        p = sha256_text(N.build_prompt(it[\"task\"], it[\"code\"]))\n        assert (\"A\", it[\"item_id\"], p) in cache and (\"B\", it[\"item_id\"], p) in cache, f\"no cached judge for {it['item_id']}\"\n    manual = [r for r in read_jsonl(SRC / \"audit\" / \"manual_pass.jsonl\") if r[\"item_id\"] in ids]\n\n    # check: the selection rebuilt from the materialised demo files is exactly the demo items\n    tmp = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=\"demo_check_\", dir=Path(args.out).resolve().parent))\n    try:\n        for rel, text in files.items():\n            (tmp / rel).parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n            (tmp / rel).write_text(text)\n        by_file: dict[tuple[str, str], list] = {}\n        for e in examples + side:\n            by_file.setdefault((e[\"aid\"], e[\"rel\"]), []).append(e)\n        for (aid, rel), es in by_file.items():\n            (tmp / aid / rel).parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n            with (tmp / aid / rel).open(\"w\") as fh:\n                for e in sorted(es, key=lambda x: x[\"line\"]):\n                    fh.write(json.dumps(e[\"record\"]) + \"\\n\")\n        os.environ[\"AII_DEPS_ROOT\"] = str(tmp)\n        demo_rows = list(iter_rows(include_exp12_extra=True))\n        dsel = N.build_selection(demo_rows)\n        got = [it[\"item_id\"] for it in dsel[\"A\"] + dsel[\"B_TOP\"] + dsel[\"B\"]]\n        assert sorted(got) == sorted(order), \"selection on the demo files differs from the demo items\"\n        n_demo_multi = Counter(r[\"condition\"] for r in demo_rows if r[\"exp\"] == \"exp9\" and r[\"condition\"] in MULTI)\n        assert n_demo_multi == n_multi, (n_demo_multi, n_multi)\n        print(f\"check passed: the demo files reproduce the 100 selected items and all {sum(n_multi.values())} \"\n              f\"multi-ban rows; {len(cache)} cached judge responses cover every item and control\")\n    finally:\n        os.environ.pop(\"AII_DEPS_ROOT\", None)\n        shutil.rmtree(tmp, ignore_errors=True)\n\n    na = read_json(SRC / \"results\" / \"novelty_audit.json\")\n    jr = read_json(SRC / \"results\" / \"novelty_judge_run.json\")\n    full_items = {r[\"item_id\"]: {k: r.get(k) for k in ITEM_KEYS} for r in na[\"table\"] if r[\"item_id\"] in order}\n    meta = {\n        \"description\": (\"Demo data for the novelty audit (section 0c) of the iteration-3 Step 0 evaluation \"\n                        \"(art_3tH2xtoZ7Gj2, eval.py). Each example is one per-program record of an origin file, reduced \"\n                        \"to the fields common/rows.py: normalise() reads; 'aid'/'rel' name the origin file \"\n                        \"(<artifact_id>/<relative path>) and 'line' its line in the full file.\"),\n        \"selection\": {\"rule\": \"full Set A (78) + full Set B_TOP (12) + 10 hand-picked Set B items (of 138)\",\n                      \"B_pick\": B_PICK, \"pinned\": \"exp9 multi-ban programs (always kept: the avoidance tally needs them)\",\n                      \"full_run_counts_unique\": sel[\"counts_unique\"], \"full_run_rows\": len(rows)},\n        \"raw_fields_kept\": list(RAW_KEYS),\n        \"files\": files,\n        \"judge_cache\": cache_rows,\n        \"manual_pass\": manual,\n        \"side_rows\": side,\n        \"side_rows_note\": (\"records of exp9 data/samples.jsonl in the conditions multi:TOP, multi:ARC, multi:SCATTER, \"\n                           \"multi:TOP5 that are not demo programs; code removed (labels, pass flags and banned sets \"\n                           \"unchanged), used only by the multi-ban avoidance tally\"),\n        \"full_run_reference\": {**{k: na[k] for k in SUMMARY_KEYS if k in na},\n                               \"judge_spend\": jr[\"spend\"], \"commit_sha256\": jr[\"commit_sha256\"],\n                               \"section_seconds\": {k: v for k, v in read_json(SRC / \"results\" / \"timings.json\").items()\n                                                   if k in (\"judges\", \"novelty\")}},\n        \"full_run_items\": full_items,\n    }\n    out = {\"metadata\": meta, \"datasets\": [{\"dataset\": \"novelty_audit_programs\", \"examples\": examples}]}\n    Path(args.out).write_text(json.dumps(out, indent=1))\n    print(f\"wrote {args.out}: {Path(args.out).stat().st_size / 1e6:.2f} MB; {'notebook_path': '/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_3/art_3tH2xtoZ7Gj2/code_demo.ipynb', 'cell_id': 'c00-title', 'edit_mode': 'replace', 'cell_type': 'markdown', 'new_source': '# Did any prompt, ban or knob make the models write a *new* algorithm? The novelty audit of `eval.py` (demo)\n\nThis notebook demonstrates the evaluation artifact **"Record repair and novelty audit of algorithm bans"** (iteration 3, Step 0). The study asks how prompt-level algorithm bans ("do not use BFS for this task") move a code model\'s choice of algorithm. The main model is **Qwen3-8B**; **Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct** is the second family. Every generated program was labelled with the algorithm it implements, out of a fixed catalogue per task:\n\n* T1, grid path: BFS, DFS, bidirectional BFS, IDDFS, A\\*, greedy best-first, IDA\\*;\n* T2, weighted shortest path: Dijkstra, Bellman-Ford/SPFA, bidirectional Dijkstra, Floyd-Warshall, Dial;\n* a 13-task coding bench (T01–T14), each task with a catalogue of its own.\n\nA program outside its task\'s catalogue is labelled `other`. The 11 earlier experiments pushed the models in many ways:\n\n* baselines;\n* single, multiple and two-step bans;\n* temperature, top-k and top-p;\n* context seeds;\n* instructions such as "make a fully novel algorithm";\n* activation steering.\n\n**The question here is whether any of those out-of-catalogue programs is a genuinely new algorithm.**\n\n`eval.py` is the artifact\'s entry point and dispatches ten sections. This demo runs the two sections of the novelty audit (0c), using the artifact\'s own code split into cells.\n\n1. **`judges`** (`sections/s0c_novelty.py`):\n   * rebuilds the audit sets from the per-program records;\n   * hash-commits the selection before any judge is consulted;\n   * collects the verdicts of two blind LLM judges from different model families, with a third judge as tie-break;\n   * includes 10 positive and 5 negative controls.\n\n   The answers come from the cache that ships with the artifact, so the notebook makes **no paid API call**.\n2. **`novelty`** (`sections/s0c_final.py`):\n   * final classes, where a manual pass overrides a judge call only with a recorded reason;\n   * Cohen\'s kappa between the two judges, with a bootstrap interval;\n   * the control gate;\n   * the novelty answer with its rule-of-three bound;\n   * the multi-ban avoidance, recomputed with `other` kept separate.\n\n**Data.** `mini_demo_data.json` holds 100 of the 228 programs audited in the full run:\n\n* all 78 of **Set A**: every unique *correct* out-of-catalogue program;\n* all 12 of **Set B_TOP**: exp9 `multi:TOP` programs outside the banned set;\n* 10 of the 138 programs in **Set B**: failing out-of-catalogue programs from ban conditions.\n\nIt also carries everything else these two sections read:\n\n* task specs and catalogue reference programs;\n* the cached judge answers and the manual-pass records;\n* label-only records of the four exp9 multi-ban conditions.\n\nThe other eight sections need data the demo does not carry: likelihood scores for `c1`, 0.95 GB of activation states for `manifold`, and all ~31,000 program records for `inventory`, `llama` and `knob`.'}
```

Every generated program was labelled with the algorithm it implements, out of a fixed catalogue per task:\\n\\n* T1, grid path: BFS, DFS, bidirectional BFS, IDDFS, A\\\\*, greedy best-first, IDA\\\\*;\\n* T2, weighted shortest path: Dijkstra, Bellman-Ford/SPFA, bidirectional Dijkstra, Floyd-Warshall, Dial;\\n* a 13-task coding bench (T01–T14), each task with a catalogue of its own.\\n\\nA program outside its task's catalogue is labelled `other`. The 11 earlier experiments pushed the models in many ways:\\n\\n* baselines;\\n* single, multiple and two-step bans;\\n* temperature, top-k and top-p;\\n* context seeds;\\n* instructions such as \\\"make a fully novel algorithm\\\";\\n* activation steering.\\n\\n**The question here is whether any of those out-of-catalogue programs is a genuinely new algorithm.**\\n\\n`eval.py` is the artifact's entry point and dispatches ten sections. This demo runs the two sections of the novelty audit (0c), using the artifact's own code split into cells.\\n\\n1. **`judges`** (`sections/s0c_novelty.py`):\\n   * rebuilds the audit sets from the per-program records;\\n   * hash-commits the selection before any judge is consulted;\\n   * collects the verdicts of two blind LLM judges from different model families, with a third judge as tie-break;\\n   * includes 10 positive and 5 negative controls.\\n\\n   The answers come from the cache that ships with the artifact, so the notebook makes **no paid API call**.\\n2. **`novelty`** (`sections/s0c_final.py`):\\n   * final classes, where a manual pass overrides a judge call only with a recorded reason;\\n   * Cohen's kappa between the two judges, with a bootstrap interval;\\n   * the control gate;\\n   * the novelty answer with its rule-of-three bound;\\n   * the multi-ban avoidance, recomputed with `other` kept separate.\\n\\n**Data.** `mini_demo_data.json` holds 100 of the 228 programs audited in the full run:\\n\\n* all 78 of **Set A**: every unique *correct* out-of-catalogue program;\\n* all 12 of **Set B_TOP**: exp9 `multi:TOP` programs outside the banned set;\\n* 10 of the 138 programs in **Set B**: failing out-of-catalogue programs from ban conditions.\\n\\nIt also carries everything else these two sections read:\\n\\n* task specs and catalogue reference programs;\\n* the cached judge answers and the manual-pass records;\\n* label-only records of the four exp9 multi-ban conditions.\\n\\nThe other eight sections need data the demo does not carry: likelihood scores for `c1`, 0.95 GB of activation states for `manifold`, and all ~31,000 program records for `inventory`, `llama` and `knob`.\"\n  }\n ],\n \"metadata\": {\n  \"kernelspec\": {\n   \"display_name\": \"Python 3\",\n   \"language\": \"python\",\n   \"name\": \"python3\"\n  },\n  \"language_info\": {\n   \"name\": \"python\",\n   \"version\": \"3.12\"\n  }\n },\n \"nbformat\": 4,\n \"nbformat_minor\": 5\n}"}

The novelty audit of `eval.py` (demo)\\n\\nThis notebook demonstrates the evaluation artifact **\\\"Record repair and novelty audit of algorithm bans\\\"** (iteration 3, Step 0). The study asks how prompt-level algorithm bans (\\\"do not use BFS for this task\\\") move a code model's choice of algorithm. The main model is **Qwen3-8B**; **Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct** is the second family. Every generated program was labelled with the algorithm it implements, out of a fixed catalogue per task:\\n\\n* T1, grid path: BFS, DFS, bidirectional BFS, IDDFS, A\\\\*, greedy best-first, IDA\\\\*;\\n* T2, weighted shortest path: Dijkstra, Bellman-Ford/SPFA, bidirectional Dijkstra, Floyd-Warshall, Dial;\\n* a 13-task coding bench (T01–T14), each task with a catalogue of its own.\\n\\nA program outside its task's catalogue is labelled `other`. The 11 earlier experiments pushed the models in many ways:\\n\\n* baselines;\\n* single, multiple and two-step bans;\\n* temperature, top-k and top-p;\\n* context seeds;\\n* instructions such as \\\"make a fully novel algorithm\\\";\\n* activation steering.\\n\\n**The question here is whether any of those out-of-catalogue programs is a genuinely new algorithm.**\\n\\n`eval.py` is the artifact's entry point and dispatches ten sections. This demo runs the two sections of the novelty audit (0c), using the artifact's own code split into cells.\\n\\n1. **`judges`** (`sections/s0c_novelty.py`):\\n   * rebuilds the audit sets from the per-program records;\\n   * hash-commits the selection before any judge is consulted;\\n   * collects the verdicts of two blind LLM judges from different model families, with a third judge as tie-break;\\n   * includes 10 positive and 5 negative controls.\\n\\n   The answers come from the cache that ships with the artifact, so the notebook makes **no paid API call**.\\n2. **`novelty`** (`sections/s0c_final.py`):\\n   * final classes, where a manual pass overrides a judge call only with a recorded reason;\\n   * Cohen's kappa between the two judges, with a bootstrap interval;\\n   * the control gate;\\n   * the novelty answer with its rule-of-three bound;\\n   * the multi-ban avoidance, recomputed with `other` kept separate.\\n\\n**Data.** `mini_demo_data.json` holds 100 of the 228 programs audited in the full run:\\n\\n* all 78 of **Set A**: every unique *correct* out-of-catalogue program;\\n* all 12 of **Set B_TOP**: exp9 `multi:TOP` programs outside the banned set;\\n* 10 The main model is **Qwen3-8B**; **Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct** is the second family. Every generated program was labelled with the algorithm it implements, out of a fixed catalogue per task:\\n\\n* T1, grid path: BFS, DFS, bidirectional BFS, IDDFS, A\\\\*, greedy best-first, IDA\\\\*;\\n* T2, weighted shortest path: Dijkstra, Bellman-Ford/SPFA, bidirectional Dijkstra, Floyd-Warshall, Dial;\\n* a 13-task coding bench (T01–T14), each task with a catalogue of its own.\\n\\nA program outside its task's catalogue is labelled `other`. The 11 earlier experiments pushed the models in many ways:\\n\\n* baselines;\\n* single, multiple and two-step bans;\\n* temperature, top-k and top-p;\\n* context seeds;\\n* instructions such as \\\"make a fully novel algorithm\\\";\\n* activation steering.\\n\\n**The question here is whether any of those out-of-catalogue programs is a genuinely new algorithm.**\\n\\n`eval.py` is the artifact's entry point and dispatches ten sections. This demo runs the two sections of the novelty audit (0c), using the artifact's own code split into cells.\\n\\n1. **`judges`** (`sections/s0c_novelty.py`):\\n   * rebuilds the audit sets from the per-program records;\\n   * hash-commits the selection before any judge is consulted;\\n   * collects the verdicts of two blind LLM judges from different model families, with a third judge as tie-break;\\n   * includes 10 positive and 5 negative controls.\\n\\n   The answers come from the cache that ships with the artifact, so the notebook makes **no paid API call**.\\n2. **`novelty`** (`sections/s0c_final.py`):\\n   * final classes, where a manual pass overrides a judge call only with a recorded reason;\\n   * Cohen's kappa between the two judges, with a bootstrap interval;\\n   * the control gate;\\n   * the novelty answer with its rule-of-three bound;\\n   * the multi-ban avoidance, recomputed with `other` kept separate.\\n\\n**Data.** `mini_demo_data.json` holds 100 of the 228 programs audited in the full run:\\n\\n* all 78 of **Set A**: every unique *correct* out-of-catalogue program;\\n* all 12 of **Set B_TOP**: exp9 `multi:TOP` programs outside the banned set;\\n* 10 of the 138 programs in **Set B**: failing out-of-catalogue programs from ban conditions.\\n\\nIt also carries everything else these two sections read:\\n\\n* task specs and catalogue reference programs;\\n* the cached judge answers and the manual-pass records;\\n* label-only records of the four exp9 multi-ban conditions.\\n\\nThe other eight sections need data the demo does not carry: likelihood scores for `c1`, 0.95 GB of activation states for `manifold`, and all ~31,000 program records for `inventory`, `llama` and `knob`.\"\n  },\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"code\",\n   \"id\": The novelty audit of `eval.py` (demo)\\n\\nThis notebook demonstrates the evaluation artifact **\\\"Record repair and novelty audit of algorithm bans\\\"** (iteration 3, Step 0). The study asks how prompt-level algorithm bans (\\\"do not use BFS for this task\\\") move a code model's choice of algorithm. The main model is **Qwen3-8B**; **Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct** is the second family. Every generated program was labelled with the algorithm it implements, out of a fixed catalogue per task:\\n\\n* T1, grid path: BFS, DFS, bidirectional BFS, IDDFS, A\\\\*, greedy best-first, IDA\\\\*;\\n* T2, weighted shortest path: Dijkstra, Bellman-Ford/SPFA, bidirectional Dijkstra, Floyd-Warshall, Dial;\\n* a 13-task coding bench (T01–T14), each task with a catalogue of its own.\\n\\nA program outside its task's catalogue is labelled `other`. The 11 earlier experiments pushed the models in many ways:\\n\\n* baselines;\\n* single, multiple and two-step bans;\\n* temperature, top-k and top-p;\\n* context seeds;\\n* instructions such as \\\"make a fully novel algorithm\\\";\\n* activation steering.\\n\\n**The question here is whether any of those out-of-catalogue programs is a genuinely new algorithm.**\\n\\n`eval.py` is the artifact's entry point and dispatches ten sections. This demo runs the two sections of the novelty audit (0c), using the artifact's own code split into cells.\\n\\n1. **`judges`** (`sections/s0c_novelty.py`):\\n   * rebuilds the audit sets from the per-program records;\\n   * hash-commits the selection before any judge is consulted;\\n   * collects the verdicts of two blind LLM judges from different model families, with a third judge as tie-break;\\n   * includes 10 positive and 5 negative controls.\\n\\n   The answers come from the cache that ships with the artifact, so the notebook makes **no paid API call**.\\n2. **`novelty`** (`sections/s0c_final.py`):\\n   * final classes, where a manual pass overrides a judge call only with a recorded reason;\\n   * Cohen's kappa between the two judges, with a bootstrap interval;\\n   * the control gate;\\n   * the novelty answer with its rule-of-three bound;\\n   * the multi-ban avoidance, recomputed with `other` kept separate.\\n\\n**Data.** `mini_demo_data.json` holds 100 of the 228 programs audited in the full run:\\n\\n* all 78 of **Set A**: every unique *correct* out-of-catalogue program;\\n* all 12 of **Set B_TOP**: exp9 `multi:TOP` programs outside the banned set;\\n* 10 of the 138 programs in **Set B**: failing out-of-catalogue programs from ban conditions.\\n\\nIt also carries everything else these two sections read:\\n\\n* task specs and catalogue reference {'notebook_path': '/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_3/art_3tH2xtoZ7Gj2/code_demo.ipynb', 'cell_id': 'cc8ca4cd', 'edit_mode': 'insert', 'cell_type': 'code', 'new_source': 'DEMO_WS = Path("eval_demo_ws").resolve()      # stands in for the artifact\'s folder (eval.py\'s WS)\nshutil.rmtree(DEMO_WS, ignore_errors=True)    # start from an empty workspace on every run\nDEPS_DIR = DEMO_WS / "deps"\nos.environ["AII_DEPS_ROOT"] = str(DEPS_DIR)   # common/paths.py: dep_root() looks here first\n\n# 1. dependency files, verbatim: task specs, catalogue reference programs, bench specs ("<artifact_id>/<path>")\nfor rel, text in META["files"].items():\n    (DEPS_DIR / rel).parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    (DEPS_DIR / rel).write_text(text)\n\n# 2. origin files: the demo programs (the first MAX_PROGRAMS, plus every pinned one) and the label-only multi-ban\n#    records, one JSONL file per origin file, in their original order\nuse = [e for i, e in enumerate(EXAMPLES) if i < MAX_PROGRAMS or e["pinned"]]\nby_file = defaultdict(list)\nfor e in use + META["side_rows"]:\n    by_file[(e["aid"], e["rel"])].append(e)\nfor (aid, rel), es in sorted(by_file.items()):\n    (DEPS_DIR / aid / rel).parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    with (DEPS_DIR / aid / rel).open("w") as fh:\n        for e in sorted(es, key=lambda x: x["line"]):\n            fh.write(json.dumps(e["record"]) + "\\n")\n\n# 3. the artifact\'s own audit files: the cached judge answers and the manual pass\n(DEMO_WS / "audit").mkdir(parents=True)\nfor name, recs in (("judge_cache.jsonl", META["judge_cache"]), ("manual_pass.jsonl", META["manual_pass"])):\n    with (DEMO_WS / "audit" / name).open("w") as fh:\n        for r in recs:\n            fh.write(json.dumps(r) + "\\n")\n(DEMO_WS / "tables").mkdir()   # the artifact ships tables/; pandas\' to_csv() does not create folders\n\nprint(f"{len(use)} demo programs {dict(Counter(e[\'set\'] for e in use))} + {len(META[\'side_rows\'])} label-only records "\n      f"in {len(by_file)} origin files:")\nfor (aid, rel), es in sorted(by_file.items()):\n    print(f"  {aid}:{rel}: {len(es)} records")'}

line:\n                try:\n                    out.append(json.loads(line))\n                except json.JSONDecodeError:\n                    continue\n    return out\n\n\ndef read_json(p: Path) -> Any:\n    return json.loads(Path(p).read_text())\n\n\ndef _default(o: Any) -> Any:\n    import numpy as np\n\n    if isinstance(o, (np.integer,)):\n        return int(o)\n    if isinstance(o, (np.floating,)):\n        v = float(o)\n        return None if not math.isfinite(v) else v\n    if isinstance(o, np.ndarray):\n        return o.tolist()\n    if isinstance(o, (set, tuple)):\n        return list(o)\n    if isinstance(o, Path):\n        return str(o)\n    return str(o)\n\n\ndef clean(o: Any) -> Any:\n    """Recursively replace non-finite floats by None so the JSON is standard."""\n    import numpy as np\n\n    if isinstance(o, dict):\n        return {str(k): clean(v) for k, v in o.items()}\n    if isinstance(o, (list, tuple)):\n        return [clean(v) for v in o]\n    if isinstance(o, (float, np.floating)):\n        v = float(o)\n        return v if math.isfinite(v) else None\n    if isinstance(o, np.integer):\n        return int(o)\n    if isinstance(o, np.ndarray):\n        return clean(o.tolist())\n    if isinstance(o, np.bool_):\n        return bool(o)\n    return o\n\n\ndef write_json(p: Path, obj: Any, indent: int | None = 1) -> None:\n    p = Path(p)\n    p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    tmp = p.with_suffix(p.suffix + ".tmp")\n    tmp.write_text(json.dumps(clean(obj), indent=indent, default=_default))\n    tmp.replace(p)\n\n\ndef write_jsonl(p: Path, rows: Iterable[dict[str, Any]]) -> None:\n    p = Path(p)\n    p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    with p.open("w") as fh:\n        for r in rows:\n            fh.write(json.dumps(clean(r), default=_default) + "\\n")\n\n\ndef sha256_file(p: Path) -> str:\n    h = hashlib.sha256()\n    with Path(p).open("rb") as fh:\n        for chunk in iter(lambda: fh.read(1 << 20), b""):\n            h.update(chunk)\n    return h.hexdigest()\n\n\ndef sha256_text(s: str) -> str:\n    return hashlib.sha256(s.encode()).hexdigest()\n\n\ndef code_key(code: str) -> str:\n    """De-duplication key: sha256 of the code with trailing whitespace stripped from every line and the ends."""\n    norm = "\\n".join(line.rstrip() for line in (code or "").strip().splitlines())\n    return sha256_text(norm)'}

{'notebook_path': '/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_3/art_3tH2xtoZ7Gj2/code_demo.ipynb', 'cell_id': '61a7678e', 'edit_mode': 'insert', 'cell_type': 'code', 'new_source': '# common/stats.py (the two functions the novelty sections use)\n# """Small statistics toolkit: Wilson / Newcombe intervals, Hill numbers (plug-in, Miller-Madow, rarefied),\n# Cohen\'s kappa, Kish ESS and the PSIS generalized-Pareto k-hat (Zhang & Stephens 2009, as used by Vehtari et al.)."""\n\n\ndef wilson(k: int, n: int, conf: float = 0.95) -> tuple[float, float]:\n    if n <= 0:\n        return (math.nan, math.nan)\n    z = norm.ppf(1 - (1 - conf) / 2)\n    p = k / n\n    den = 1 + z * z / n\n    c = (p + z * z / (2 * n)) / den\n    h = z * math.sqrt(p * (1 - p) / n + z * z / (4 * n * n)) / den\n    return (max(0.0, c - h), min(1.0, c + h))\n\n\ndef cohen_kappa(a: Sequence[str], b: Sequence[str]) -> float:\n    a, b = list(a), list(b)\n    n = len(a)\n    if n == 0:\n        return math.nan\n    cats = sorted(set(a) | set(b))\n    po = sum(x == y for x, y in zip(a, b)) / n\n    ca, cb = Counter(a), Counter(b)\n    pe = sum(ca[c] * cb[c] for c in cats) / (n * n)\n    return float((po - pe) / (1 - pe)) if pe < 1 else (1.0 if po == 1 else math.nan)'}
```

A judge sees only three things:\n\n* the task specification;\n* an *extended* catalogue with one line of mechanism per known algorithm. It includes textbook neighbours outside the study's labels: JPS, bidirectional A\\*, Lee wavefront, beam search, Johnson, 0-1 BFS, DAG dynamic programming, and others;\n* the code.\n\nIt answers with one of four classes:\n\n| class | meaning |\n|---|---|\n| `LABELLER_MISS` | the program *is* a catalogue algorithm, possibly renamed or disguised |\n| `CATALOGUE_VARIANT` | a hybrid or variant of a catalogue or textbook algorithm, which the judge must name |\n| `NOVEL_ALGORITHM` | the mechanism matches no catalogue or textbook algorithm the judge knows |\n| `DEGENERATE_OR_HARDCODED` | brute-force enumeration, special-casing of inputs, or literal outputs |\n\n`INSTRUCTION_CONDS` flags the conditions that explicitly asked for something new: `instr_*`, `creative`, `new_algo`, `fully_novel`, enumeration, steering, `decommit`, `nonce`, removal."}

no heuristic bound.",\n    "A*": "a priority queue (heap) ordered by f = g + h, cost so far plus a heuristic such as the Manhattan distance to the goal; one search direction.",\n    "greedy best-first search": "a priority queue ordered by the heuristic h alone (e.g. Manhattan distance to the goal), ignoring the cost so far; A* with a non-zero heuristic; 0-1 BFS / deque-based shortest path; BFS ignoring weights; exhaustive DFS enumeration of all simple paths; memoised DFS over (node) states.",\n}\nINSTRUCTION_CONDS = re.compile(r"(instr_|creative|new_algo|fully_novel|enumerat|steer:dc|decommit|novelty|nonce|removal:)",\n                               re.I)\n\nSYSTEM = ("You are an expert in algorithms and program analysis. You classify the algorithmic MECHANISM of a Python "\n          "program written for a stated task. You are not told who wrote it or why. Answer with one JSON object only.")'}

They are stratified by (experiment, task, model, ban family), with at least 5 per stratum where available, and shuffled with the global seed.\n\nOn the demo records the same code selects exactly the demo programs. This holds because Set A and B_TOP are complete here, and because the budget covers all 10 Set B programs.'}

The expected answer is `DEGENERATE_OR_HARDCODED`.\n\nThe gate passes with at least 9/10 positives and at least 4/5 negatives.'}

                 ast.Constant):\n        tree.body = tree.body[1:]\n    tree = _Renamer().visit(tree)\n    ast.fix_missing_locations(tree)\n    return ast.unparse(tree)\n\n\nPOS_CONTROLS = [("t1", "bfs_deque_parent.py", "bfs"), ("t1", "dfs_stack.py", "dfs"), ("t1", "bibfs_sets.py", "bibfs"),\n                ("t1", "iddfs_iterative.py", "iddfs"), ("t1", "astar_fg.py", "astar"),\n                ("t2", "dijkstra_lazy.py", "dijkstra"), ("t2", "bellman_ford.py", "bf_spfa"),\n                ("t2", "floyd_inf.py", "floyd"), ("t2", "dial_list.py", "dial"),\n                ("t2", "bidijkstra_alternate.py", "bidijkstra")]\n\nNEG_CONTROLS = [\n    ("t1", "def solve(grid, start, goal):\\n    if start == goal:\\n        return [start]\\n    known = {((0, 0), (0, 2)): [(0, 0), (0, 1), (0, 2)], ((0, 0), (2, 0)): [(0, 0), (1, 0), (2, 0)]}\\n    if (tuple(start), tuple(goal)) in known:\\n        return known[(tuple(start), tuple(goal))]\\n    if len(grid) == 1 and len(grid[0]) == 2:\\n        return [start, goal]\\n    return []\\n"),\n    ("t1", "import itertools\\n\\ndef solve(grid, start, goal):\\n    free = [(r, c) for r in range(len(grid)) for c in range(len(grid[0])) if grid[r][c] == \'.\']\\n    if start not in free or goal not in free:\\n        return []\\n    if start == goal:\\n        return [start]\\n    middle = [p for p in free if p != start and p != goal]\\n    for k in range(len(middle) + 1):\\n        for combo in itertools.permutations(middle, k):\\n            path = [start, *combo, goal]\\n            if all(abs(a[0] - b[0]) + abs(a[1] - b[1]) == 1 for a, b in zip(path, path[1:])):\\n                return list(path)\\n    return []\\n"),\n    ("t2", "def solve(n, edges, s, t):\\n    table = {(2, 0, 1): 5, (3, 0, 2): 7, (4, 0, 3): 9}\\n    if s == t:\\n        return 0\\n    if (n, s, t) in table:\\n        return table[(n, s, t)]\\n    if not edges:\\n        return -1\\n    return min(w for (u, v, w) in edges if u == s and v == t) if any(u == s and v == t for u, v, _ in edges) else -1\\n"),\n    ("t2", "import itertools\\n\\ndef solve(n, edges, s, t):\\n    if s == t:\\n        return 0\\n    w = {}\\n    for u, v, c in edges:\\n        if (u, v) not in w or c < w[(u, v)]:\\n            w[(u, v)] = c\\n    best = -1\\n    others = [x for x in range(n) if x not in (s, t)]\\n    for k in range(len(others) + 1):\\n        for mid in itertools.permutations(others, k):\\n            path = [s, *mid, t]\\n            if all((a, b) in w for a, b in zip(path, path[1:])):\\n                cost = sum(w[(a, b)] for a, b in zip(path, path[1:]))\\n                if best < 0 or cost < best:\\n                    best = cost\\n    return best\\n"),\n    ("t2", "def solve(n, edges, s, t):\\n    if s == t:\\n        return 0\\n    answers = {0: -1, 1: 4, 2: 10, 3: 17, 4: 3}\\n    return answers.get((n * 7 + len(edges) * 3 + s + t) % 5, -1)\\n"),\n]\n\n\ndef build_controls() -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n    out = []\n    for task, fname, lab in POS_CONTROLS:\n        d = "t1_grid" if task == "t1" else "t2_wsp"\n        code = (dep("exp1", f"tasks/{d}/references/{fname}")).read_text()\n        out.append({"item_id": f"CTRL_POS|{task}|{lab}", "set": "control_pos", "task": task, "code": rename_code(code),\n                    "expected_class": "LABELLER_MISS", "expected_match": lab,\n                    "source": src("exp1", f"tasks/{d}/references/{fname}")})\n    for i, (task, code) in enumerate(NEG_CONTROLS):\n        out.append({"item_id": f"CTRL_NEG|{task}|{i}", "set": "control_neg", "task": task, "code": code,\n                    "expected_class": "DEGENERATE_OR_HARDCODED", "expected_match": None, "source": "hand-written"})\n    return out'}

The program may or may not be correct; correctness does not matter.\\n\\n"\n            f"PROGRAM\\n```python\\n{code.strip()}\\n```\\n\\n"\n            "Return one JSON object with exactly these keys: \\"class\\" (one of LABELLER_MISS, CATALOGUE_VARIANT, "\n            "NOVEL_ALGORITHM, DEGENERATE_OR_HARDCODED), \\"catalogue_match\\" (the catalogue entry name, or null), "\n            "\\"closest_known_algorithm\\" (string), \\"mechanism_summary\\" (at most 40 words), \\"rationale\\" (at most 80 "\n            "words), \\"confidence\\" (number between 0 and 1).")'}

async with sem:\n        if budget.stop or budget.spent >= COST_STOP_USD:   # checked AFTER acquiring the slot\n            budget.stop = True\n            return None\n        extra: dict[str, Any] =    return None\n            try:\n                t0 = time.time()\n                resp = await client.chat.completions.create(\n                    model=model, temperature=0, max_tokens=400, getattr(u, "cost", None)\n                    if cost is None and getattr(u, "model_extra", None):\n                        cost = u.model_extra.get("cost")\n                    if cost is None:\n                        pi, po = PRICE[model]\n                        cost = (u.prompt_tokens * pi + u.completion_tokens * po) / 1e6\n                cost = float(cost or 0.0)\n                budget.spent += cost\n                budget.n_calls += 1\n                parsed = _parse(text)\n                rec = {"judge": judge, "model": model, "item_id": item["item_id"], "prompt_sha": "prompt_tokens", None), "completion_tokens":\n                           getattr(u, "completion_tokens", None), "utc": time.strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ", time.gmtime())}\n                cache_fh.write(json.dumps(rec) + "\\n")\n                cache_fh.flush()\n                logger.debug(f"judge {judge} {item[\'item_id\']} -> {parsed and parsed.get(\'class\')} ${cost:.5f} "\n call")\n                    return None\n                logger.warning(f"judge {judge} {item[\'item_id\']} attempt {attempt + 1} failed: {msg[:200]}")\n                await asyncio.sleep(2 * cache = load_cache() if reuse_cache else {}\n    spent0 = sum(r.get("cost", 0.0) for r in cache.values())\n    budget = Budget(spent0)\n    client = AsyncOpenAI(base_url=os.environ["OPENROUTER_BASE_URL"], api_key=os.environ["OPENROUTER_API_KEY"])\n    sem = asyncio.Semaphore(max_conc)\n    results: dict[str, dict[str, dict[str, Any]]] = defaultdict(dict)\n    prompts = {it["item_id"]: build_prompt(it["task"], it["code"]) for it in items}\n    AUDIT.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    with CACHE.open("a") as fh:\n        # stage 1: judges A and B on every item (priority order is the order of `items`)\n        tasks = []\n        for it in items:\n            for j in ("A", "B"):\n                key = (j, it["item_id"], sha256_text(prompts[it["item_id"]]))\n                if key in cache and cache[key].get("parsed"):\n                    results[it["item_id"]][j] = cache[key]\n                else:\n                    tasks.append((it, j))\n        logger.info(f"judge stage 1: {len(tasks)} calls to make ({len(items)} items; cache spent ${spent0:.4f})")\n        recs = await asyncio.gather(*[_call(client, sem, budget, j, it, prompts[it["item_id"]], fh)\n                                      for it, j in tasks], return_exceptions=True)\n        for (it, j), rec in zip(tasks, recs):\n            if isinstance(rec, dict):\n                results[it["item_id"]][j] = rec\n        # stage 2: tie-break C where A and B disagree on class\n        tasks = []\n        for it in items:\n            r = results.get(it["item_id"], {})\n            a = (r.get("A") or {}).get("parsed") or {}\n            b = (r.get("B") or {}).get("parsed") or {}\n            if a.get("class") and b.get("class") and a["class"] != b["class"]:\n                key = ("C", it["item_id"], sha256_text(prompts[it["item_id"]]))\n                if key in cache and cache[key].get("parsed"):\n                    results[it["item_id"]]["C"] = cache[key]\n                else:\n                    tasks.append((it, "C"))\n        logger.info(f"judge stage 2 (tie-break): {len(tasks)} calls")\n        recs = await asyncio.gather(*[_call(client, sem, budget, j, it, prompts[it["item_id"]], fh)\n                                      for it, j in tasks], return_exceptions=True)\n        for (it, j), rec in zip(tasks, recs):\n            if isinstance(rec, dict):\n                results[it["item_id"]][j] = rec\n    return {"results": dict(results), "spent_usd_total_in_cache": budget.spent, "n_new_calls": budget.n_calls,\n            "stopped": budget.stop, "stop_reason": budget.reason}'}

Otherwise C's class is final, and the record says whether C sided with A, sided with B, or decided a three-way split.\n* **`score_controls()`.** A positive control is a hit when two things hold: its final class is `LABELLER_MISS`, and the name the deciding judge gave maps to the expected algorithm, through `match_name()`'s keyword rules. Negative controls must come back `DEGENERATE_OR_HARDCODED`. With `shuffle_expected=True`, the expected names are permuted; that run is a placebo, which must fail.\n* **`ast_shingles()` and `max_jaccard()`.** These measure structural closeness. A program becomes the set of 4-grams of the AST node types it contains (attributes and method calls are named). Its Jaccard similarity is then taken against every catalogue reference program of its task, and the maximum is kept."}
```

SyntaxError:\n        return set()\n    toks = []\n    for n in ast.walk(tree):\n        name = type(n).__name__\n        if isinstance(n, ast.Attribute):\n            name = f"Attr.{n.attr}"\n        elif isinstance(n, ast.Call) and isinstance(n.func, ast.Attribute):\n            name = f"Call.{n.func.attr}"\n        toks.append(name)\n    return {tuple(toks[i:i + 4]) for i in range(max(0, len(toks) - 3))}\n\n\ndef reference_shingles() -> dict[str, dict[str, set]]:\n    out: dict[str, dict[str, set]] = {}\n    for task, d in (("t1", "t1_grid"), ("t2", "t2_wsp")):\n        out[task] = {}\n        for p in sorted(dep("exp1", f"tasks/{d}/references").glob("*.py")):\n            out[task][p.stem] = ast_shingles(p.read_text())\n        for p in sorted(dep("exp9", f"tasks/{d}/references").glob("*.py")):\n            out[task].setdefault(f"exp9:{p.stem}", ast_shingles(p.read_text()))\n    for d in dep("exp11", "bench").iterdir():\n        if d.is_dir() and (d / "references").exists():\n            t = d.name.split("_")[0]\n            out[t] = {p.stem: ast_shingles(p.read_text()) for p in sorted((d / "references").glob("*.py"))}\n    return out\n\n\ndef max_jaccard(code: str, task: str, refs: dict[str, dict[str, set]]) -> tuple[float, str | None]:\n    s = ast_shingles(code)\n    best, arg = 0.0, None\n    for name, r in refs.get(task, {}).items():\n        if not s or not r:\n            continue\n        j = len(s & r) / len(s | r)\n        if j > best:\n            best, arg = j, name\n    return best, arg'}

The executor\'s manual pass replaces it only where it records `override` with a reason.\n2. **Agreement.** It computes Cohen\'s kappa between A and B, with a 2,000-resample bootstrap 95% interval, the A×B confusion table, kappa per set, and a collapsed kappa over KNOWN / NOVEL / DEGENERATE.\n3. **Controls.** It scores them twice: as they are, and as a placebo with the expected names shuffled, which must fail.\n4. **The novelty answer.** It counts the Set A programs whose *final* class is `NOVEL_ALGORITHM`. If that count is zero, the **rule of three** gives 3/n as an approximate 95% upper bound on the rate of novel programs in that population.\n5. **Class counts** by condition, model and task, with the instruction-driven conditions flagged.\n6. **Multi-ban avoidance.** For each of exp9\'s multiple-ban prompts it counts how many of the 100 programs avoid the banned set, and splits them into non-banned catalogue algorithms, `other` and unparseable. An `other` program that the judges identify as a banned algorithm is moved back to "banned". The four prompts:\n\n| prompt | banned algorithms |\n|---|---|\n| TOP | BFS, DFS, greedy best-first, IDDFS |\n| ARC | A\\*, BFS, bidirectional BFS, greedy best-first |\n| SCATTER | BFS, DFS, greedy best-first, IDA\\* |\n| TOP5 | A\\*, BFS, DFS, greedy best-first, IDDFS |\n\nOne notebook change: the kappa bootstrap size is `N_KAPPA_BOOT` from the configuration cell; the original hard-codes 2000.'}

set_src("1c1c3a77", r'''## Summary

With all 100 demo programs, the artifact's own code reproduces the full run's novelty answer and checks it program by program.

* **Every program agrees with the full run.** All 100 programs and all 15 controls found their cached judge answers under the sha256 of the rebuilt prompt, so the demo reconstructs the full run's prompts byte for byte. For each program, the three judges' classes, the decision path, the final class and the AST similarity all equal the full run's (100/100).
* **No correct program is a new algorithm.** Set A, all 78 correct out-of-catalogue programs, is complete in the demo.
  * 65 of them are a catalogue algorithm the labeller missed, most often a unit-weight Dijkstra on the grid or a counting-sort selection on bench task T03.
  * The other 13 are named variants, such as SPFA without an in-queue flag, or Dijkstra and BFS hybrids.
  * Only one of the 78 drew a `NOVEL` call from any judge: gpt-4.1-mini, on that SPFA variant. Both other judges named the algorithm.
  * With 0 novel programs out of 78, the rule of three bounds the novel rate in this population at **3/78 = 0.038** (95%).
  * 62 of the 78 programs come from prompts that *forced* a particular algorithm ("write A\*", "write a unit-weight Dijkstra"), not from requests to be creative.
  * The four programs from conditions flagged as asking for something new all come from activation steering, and all four are known algorithms.
* **"Novel" is what the judges call broken code.**
  * The judges called 4 of the 10 failing Set B programs `NOVEL` (17 of 138 in the full run).
  * On reading, each is a degenerate program or a broken catalogue variant: a garbled path search, a recursive DFS wrapped in a heap, backtracking from the goal, a memoised DFS. Each override records its reason.
* **The judges are not reliable enough to use alone.**
  * Agreement between A and B is moderate: kappa 0.43 [0.28, 0.58] on these 100 programs, and 0.38 [0.31, 0.45] on all 228.
  * The positive controls fail the strict gate at 7/10, because IDDFS, Dial and bidirectional Dijkstra came back as "variants", even though both judges named the right algorithm in 10/10.
  * The negative controls pass 5/5, and the shuffled-names placebo scores 0/10, as it must.
* **Banning the most common algorithms does not push the model somewhere new.** The exp9 multi-ban prompts give:

| prompt | still use a banned algorithm | avoid the banned set | what the avoiders write |
|---|---|---|---|
| TOP | 87% | 13% | 12 degenerate failing programs and one unit-weight Dijkstra |
| ARC | 63% | 37% | all of them a non-banned catalogue algorithm |
| SCATTER | 96% | 4% | |
| TOP5 | 100% | 0% | |

**What the demo leaves out.**

* Its Set B holds 10 of the 138 programs, so its Set B counts, overall kappa and cache spend differ from the full run by construction.
* The other eight sections of `eval.py` need data the demo does not carry: likelihood scores, activation states, and all 31,269 records.
* To run the full evaluation, use `uv run eval.py` in the artifact folder. Its `reproducibility.md` lists the 11 dependency artifacts.''')

with open(p, "w") as fh:
    json.dump(nb, fh, indent=1, sort_keys=True, ensure_ascii=False)
    fh.write("\n")
print("ok")
EOF

### [238] TOOL CALL — Write · 2026-10-26 10:23:38 UTC

````
File: /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_3/art_3tH2xtoZ7Gj2/README.md

# Demo: did any prompt, ban or knob make the models write a *new* algorithm?

This folder turns an evaluation artifact into a notebook. The artifact is *"Record repair and novelty audit of algorithm bans"*: round 3, evaluation 2, entry point `eval.py`. The notebook runs top to bottom in about 25 seconds on a 2-CPU machine, on Google Colab or in local Jupyter.

**The study.** It asks how prompt-level algorithm bans ("do not use BFS for this task") move a code model's choice of algorithm. The main model is Qwen3-8B; Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct is the second model family. Every generated program carries a label: the catalogue algorithm it implements, for the grid path task, the weighted shortest path task or a 13-task coding bench, or `other` when it lies outside the catalogue. The earlier experiments pushed the models in many ways:

* bans;
* decoding settings;
* context seeds;
* "write a novel algorithm" instructions;
* activation steering.

**The question of the demo.** Is any of the out-of-catalogue programs a genuinely new algorithm?

**What the notebook runs.** `eval.py` dispatches ten sections. The notebook runs the two sections of the novelty audit (0c), with the artifact's own code split into cells and an explanation before each one.

* **`judges`** (`sections/s0c_novelty.py`)
  * rebuilds the audit sets from the per-program records;
  * hash-commits the selection before any judge is read;
  * collects the verdicts of two blind LLM judges (openai/gpt-4.1-mini and google/gemini-2.5-flash), with a tie-break (deepseek/deepseek-chat), plus 10 positive and 5 negative controls.

  The answers come from the artifact's judge cache, so the notebook makes **no paid API call**. Setting `CALL_JUDGES = True` re-enables the OpenRouter path.
* **`novelty`** (`sections/s0c_final.py`)
  * final classes, where a manual pass overrides only with a recorded reason;
  * Cohen's kappa between the judges, with a bootstrap interval;
  * the control gate;
  * the novelty answer with its rule-of-three bound;
  * the exp9 multi-ban avoidance, with `other` kept separate.

The cells also hold the parts of `common/paths.py`, `common/rows.py` and `common/stats.py` that these two sections use, and `eval.py`'s own entry points and `main()`.

The other eight sections (`inventory`, `c1`, `manifold`, `llama`, `knob`, `positioning`, `corrections`, `assemble`) need data outside the demo. Their entry points are kept, but they are not called.

## Layout

| path | what it is |
|---|---|
| `code_demo.ipynb` | The demo notebook, saved with the outputs of a complete run in a clean Python 3.12 environment. It loads its data from this repository's raw GitHub URL, and falls back to the local file. |
| `mini_demo_data.json` | Demo data, 0.97 MB; its contents are listed below the table. |
| `build_mini_demo_data.py` | Rebuilds `mini_demo_data.json` from the artifact folder, with the artifact's own code (`common/rows.py`, `sections/s0c_novelty.py: build_selection()`). It checks that the demo files reproduce the selection and that every item and control hits the judge cache. |
| `logs/build_mini_demo_data.log` | Log of the data build. |
| `logs/nbtest_round{0,1,2}.log`, `logs/nbtest_final.log` | `jupyter nbconvert --execute` runs in a fresh virtual environment: the minimum configuration, the middle one, the full one, then the final notebook. |
| `.aii/manifest.yaml` | Storage manifest. It has no entries, because nothing in this folder is heavy. |

`mini_demo_data.json` holds 100 of the 228 programs audited in the full run:

* all 78 of Set A, every unique correct out-of-catalogue program;
* all 12 of Set B_TOP, the exp9 `multi:TOP` programs outside the banned set;
* 10 of the 138 programs in Set B, failing out-of-catalogue programs from ban conditions. These were chosen to cover several experiments, both models, and every way a final class is reached.

Each example is one per-program origin record, reduced to the fields `common/rows.py: normalise()` reads. The metadata carries everything else the two sections read:

* the label-only records of the four exp9 multi-ban conditions;
* the task specs and catalogue reference programs;
* the 264 cached judge answers (the raw response text is dropped, the parsed JSON is kept);
* the manual-pass records;
* the full run's results, for comparison.

When the notebook runs, it writes this data into the layout the original code reads (`$AII_DEPS_ROOT/<artifact_id>/…` and `audit/`) inside a working folder, `eval_demo_ws/`. The functions then run unchanged, and the folder is rebuilt on every run.

## How to run

**Colab.** Open `code_demo.ipynb` and run all cells. The first cell installs `loguru`. Everything else the notebook needs is pre-installed on Colab.

**Local, Python 3.12.**

```bash
pip install jupyter ipykernel
jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute code_demo.ipynb --output code_demo.ipynb
```

Outside Colab, the install cell also pins Colab's versions of the other packages: numpy 2.0.2, pandas 2.2.2, scipy 1.16.3, matplotlib 3.10.0 and openai 2.17.0. If the GitHub URL cannot be reached, the notebook reads the local `mini_demo_data.json`.

**Rebuilding the data** needs the evaluation artifact's folder, the one holding `eval.py`, `common/`, `sections/`, `audit/` and `results/`. In the published repository that is the parent of this folder. Its 11 dependency artifacts are found by that artifact's own `common/paths.py: dep_root()`, so if the sibling folders are not named by artifact id, set `AII_DEPS_ROOT` as its `reproducibility.md` explains. The build needs numpy, scipy and loguru:

```bash
python build_mini_demo_data.py --src .. --out mini_demo_data.json
```

## Configuration and measured runtime

All tunable parameters are in one configuration cell.

* **`MAX_PROGRAMS`** is the number of demo programs fed to the audit. The 15 exp9 multi-ban programs are always kept, because the avoidance tally needs them.
* **`N_KAPPA_BOOT`** is the bootstrap size of the kappa interval. The original hard-codes 2,000.
* **`SET_B_MAX`**, **`CALL_JUDGES`**, **`REUSE_JUDGE_CACHE`** and **`SECTIONS_TO_RUN`** are also there, together with the arguments of the sections that are not run.

The final notebook uses all 100 demo programs and the original bootstrap size of 2,000. The full run audited 228 programs drawn from 31,269 records; that is beyond the demo's data, not its time budget.

Each test run below is the wall time of `jupyter nbconvert --execute` in a fresh virtual environment on 2 CPUs, including the install cell. The two sections themselves take about 2 s.

| round | `MAX_PROGRAMS` (programs audited) | `N_KAPPA_BOOT` | runtime |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0, minimum | 0 (the 15 pinned) | 10 | 24 s |
| 1 | 40 (52) | 200 | 23 s |
| 2, final | 100 (100) | 2,000 | 23 s |

## Results: demo (default configuration) vs the full run

| quantity | demo (100 programs) | full run (228 programs) |
|---|---|---|
| audited programs, A / B_TOP / B | 78 / 12 / 10 | 78 / 12 / 138 |
| final classes, Set A (correct programs) | labeller miss 65, catalogue variant 13 | the same |
| final classes, Set B_TOP | degenerate 12 | the same |
| `NOVEL` calls by the judges, Set B | 4, all overridden on reading | 17, all overridden |
| confirmed novel correct programs | **0** | **0** |
| rule-of-three 95% bound on the novel rate, Set A | 3/78 = 0.038 | 0.038 |
| Cohen's kappa, judge A vs judge B [95% CI] | 0.43 [0.28, 0.58] | 0.38 [0.31, 0.45] |
| kappa per set, A / B_TOP / B | 0.41 / −0.13 / 0.30 | 0.41 / −0.13 / 0.24 |
| controls: positives strict, named right by both, negatives | 7/10, 10/10, 5/5 (gate fails) | the same |
| placebo with shuffled expected names | 0/10 | 0/10 |
| exp9 multi-ban avoidance, TOP / ARC / SCATTER / TOP5 | 0.13 / 0.37 / 0.04 / 0.00 | the same |

Every one of the 100 demo programs gets the same judge classes, the same decision path, the same final class and the same AST similarity as in the full run. That is only possible because the rebuilt judge prompts are byte-identical to the ones the judges saw: answers are looked up by the sha256 of the prompt.

Some numbers differ from the full run by construction, because the demo has 10 of the 138 Set B programs:

* the Set B counts;
* the overall kappa;
* the number of tie-breaks;
* the spend recorded in the cache: USD 0.19 for the demo items, against USD 0.415 for all 599 calls.

## What the notebook changes in the original code

Within the converted cells, every line of `eval.py`, `common/paths.py`, `common/rows.py`, `sections/s0c_novelty.py` and `sections/s0c_final.py` appears verbatim, along with the two `common/stats.py` functions used. The exceptions are the following, each marked `# notebook:` in the cell:

* **Docstrings and imports.** Module docstrings become comments. The import blocks are merged into one cell, and the project's own imports are replaced by the cells that define the same code.
* **`common/paths.py`.** `WS` is the demo workspace. The unused run-volume fallback `RUNS` no longer indexes five parent folders up.
* **Module stand-ins.** `s0c_novelty` and `s0c_final` stand in for the module objects, because both modules define a function named `run()`.
* **`sec_judges`.** It passes `call_judges=CALL_JUDGES`. The original passes `True`, which needs the OpenRouter keys even when everything is cached.
* **`s0c_novelty.run()`.** It runs `asyncio.run()` in a worker thread, because Jupyter already has an event loop.
* **`s0c_final.run()`.** It uses `N_KAPPA_BOOT` bootstrap resamples instead of the literal 2000.
* **`main()`.** It parses an empty command line, with the configuration cell as defaults, and runs `SECTIONS_TO_RUN`.

## Restoring removed files

Nothing in this folder was deleted, and `.aii/manifest.yaml` has no `delete` entries. Two things were created during testing and are not kept here:

* **`eval_demo_ws/`**, the notebook's working folder. It holds the demo dependency files, the audit cache, and the results and tables the two sections write. The notebook rebuilds it from `mini_demo_data.json` on every run. To restore it, run the notebook: `jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute code_demo.ipynb --output code_demo.ipynb`.
* **The test virtual environment.** It lived on local disk outside this folder. To recreate it, run `pip install jupyter ipykernel` in a Python 3.12 environment; the notebook's first cell installs everything else.
````

The original passes `True`, which needs the OpenRouter keys even when everything is cached.\n* **`s0c_novelty.run()`.** It runs `asyncio.run()` in a worker thread, because Jupyter already has an event loop.\n* **`s0c_final.run()`.** It uses `N_KAPPA_BOOT` bootstrap resamples instead of the literal 2000.\n* **`main()`.** It parses an empty command line, with the configuration cell as defaults, and runs `SECTIONS_TO_RUN`.\n\n## Restoring removed files\n\nNothing in this folder was deleted, and `.aii/manifest.yaml` has no `delete` entries. Two things were created during testing and are not kept here:\n\n* **`eval_demo_ws/`**, the notebook's working folder. It holds the demo dependency files, the audit cache, and the results and tables the two sections write. The notebook rebuilds it from `mini_demo_data.json` on every run. To restore it, run the notebook: `jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute code_demo.ipynb --output code_demo.ipynb`.\n* **The test virtual environment.** It lived on local disk outside this folder. To recreate it, run `pip install jupyter ipykernel` in a Python 3.12 environment; the notebook's first cell installs everything else.\n", "structuredPatch": [], "originalFile": null, "userModified": false}

### [250] ASSISTANT · 2026-10-26 10:24:18 UTC

```
I've built the demo notebook and its data; it runs top to bottom in a clean Python 3.12 environment in about 23 seconds with no errors.

**What it covers.** `eval.py` dispatches ten sections. Only the two novelty-audit sections can run from a small data file:
- **`judges`:** answers come from the artifact's cached LLM-judge responses, so no paid calls.
- **`novelty`:** final classes, judge agreement, controls, the novelty answer and multi-ban avoidance.

The other eight sections need data that can't fit in 100 examples (likelihood scores, 0.95 GB of activation states, all ~31,000 program records). Their entry points stay in the notebook but aren't called.

**The data.** `mini_demo_data.json` (0.97 MB) holds 100 of the 228 audited programs: all 78 correct out-of-catalogue programs, all 12 TOP-ban programs, and 10 of the 138 failing ones. I built it with the artifact's own selection code; `build_mini_demo_data.py` rebuilds it.

**Results against the full run** (all 100 programs, 2,000 bootstrap resamples):
- **Per program:** every one of the 100 gets the same judge calls and the same final class as in the full run. Every rebuilt judge prompt matched its cached answer exactly.
- **Novelty answer:** 0 of 78 correct programs is a novel algorithm, with an upper bound of 0.038 on the novel rate.
- **Exact matches:** the controls (7/10, 10/10, 5/5) and the multi-ban avoidance shares (0.13 / 0.37 / 0.04 / 0.00) both match.
- **Expected differences:** the failing-program counts, overall kappa (0.43 here, 0.38 in the full run) and judge spend differ, because the demo has only 10 of the 138 failing programs.

**Changes to the original code.** Apart from docstrings turned into comments and merged imports, every original line is in the notebook verbatim; I checked this with a line-by-line diff. The few changes are marked `# notebook:` in the cells and listed in `README.md`.

**Process notes.**
- There was no todo-tracking tool, so I tracked the six TODOs myself.
- After the first test run, the executed notebook was too large to Read, which NotebookEdit requires. Later edits were therefore made by cell id with small inline scripts.
- I treated the research brief at the end of your message as background only.

Files are in the workspace folder:
- `code_demo.ipynb`
- `mini_demo_data.json`
- `build_mini_demo_data.py`
- `README.md`
- `logs/`
- `.aii/manifest.yaml`
- `.terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json`
```
